{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_ac0437723f499fd249a1859f24eff01d0} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} \+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp\+\_\+mask}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{, }\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{caller}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory on a specific node with alignment without allocation profiling. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_adcb6460c4975e163db21e9c8d6adb01e}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node()}.

\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory @size:\+ allocation size @align:\+ desired alignment @gfp\+\_\+mask:\+ flags for the page level allocator @node:\+ node to use for allocation or NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE @caller:\+ caller\textquotesingle{}s return address

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator with @gfp\+\_\+mask flags. Map them into contiguous kernel virtual space.

Semantics of @gfp\+\_\+mask (including reclaim/\+retry modifiers such as \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOFAIL) are the same as in \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_aecfaafb3ba16c7feec7f449824ddea5c}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range\+\_\+noprof()}.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory on a specific node. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_aecfaafb3ba16c7feec7f449824ddea5c}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range\+\_\+noprof()} with VMALLOC\+\_\+\+START/\+\+END. Returns the address or NULL.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a699b8df354004dc37ced2903d4833a54} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} \+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{start}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{end}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{gfp\+\_\+mask}{, }\item[{pgprot\+\_\+t}]{prot}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{vm\+\_\+flags}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{, }\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{caller}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory within a specific address range without allocation profiling. It allows specifying start and end addresses, alignment, flags, protection, and node. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a08b352ce7783e3a20c50e7a6e8fcd612}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range()}.

\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+range -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory @size:\+ allocation size @align:\+ desired alignment @start:\+ vm area range start @end:\+ vm area range end @gfp\+\_\+mask:\+ flags for the page level allocator @prot:\+ protection mask for the allocated pages @vm\+\_\+flags:\+ additional vm area flags (e.\+g. VM\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+GUARD) @node:\+ node to use for allocation or NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE @caller:\+ caller\textquotesingle{}s return address

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator with @gfp\+\_\+mask flags and map them into contiguous virtual range with protection @prot.

Supported GFP classes:\+ GFP\+\_\+\+KERNEL, GFP\+\_\+\+ATOMIC, GFP\+\_\+\+NOWAIT, GFP\+\_\+\+NOFS and GFP\+\_\+\+NOIO. Zone modifiers are not supported. Please note GFP\+\_\+\+ATOMIC and GFP\+\_\+\+NOWAIT are supported only by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_abf4d6ac386bd660cbdd449b5cfefc11d}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc()}.

Retry modifiers:\+ only \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOFAIL is supported; \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NORETRY and \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+RETRY\+\_\+\+MAYFAIL are not supported.

\+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+NOWARN can be used to suppress failure messages.

Can not be called from interrupt nor NMI contexts. Return:\+ the address of the area or NULL on failure

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory in a range. It handles alignment, huge pages, KASAN, and retries. This is the core vmalloc allocation function.

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory with specified GFP flags without allocation profiling. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_abf4d6ac386bd660cbdd449b5cfefc11d}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc()}.

This is only for performance analysis of vmalloc and stress purpose. It is required by vmalloc test module, therefore do not use it other than that. Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory with a gfp mask. It fixes invalid flags and calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_af101d51085d249f99c8a0812d815fa9a}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+noprof()}. Returns the address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Applies the current memory allocation scope to GFP flags. This adds flags based on the current task\textquotesingle{}s memory allocation context. Returns the modified GFP flags.

Page tables allocations ignore external GFP. Enforces it by the memalloc scope API. It is used by vmalloc internals and KASAN shadow population only.

GFP to scope mapping:\+

non-\/blocking (no \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+DIRECT\+\_\+\+RECLAIM) -\/ memalloc\+\_\+noreclaim\+\_\+save() GFP\+\_\+\+NOFS -\/ memalloc\+\_\+nofs\+\_\+save() GFP\+\_\+\+NOIO -\/ memalloc\+\_\+noio\+\_\+save()

Returns a flag cookie to pair with restore.

Function Description:\+ Applies memory allocation scope based on GFP flags. It saves the current scope and applies the requested scope. Returns a cookie for restoration.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{pcpu\_get\_vm\_areas()}{pcpu\_get\_vm\_areas()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a8ac394f9dd55e5dca1705b7d761aca93} 
struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} pcpu\+\_\+get\+\_\+vm\+\_\+areas (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned long \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{offsets}{, }\item[{const size\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{sizes}{, }\item[{int}]{nr\+\_\+vms}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{align}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Allocates multiple \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} areas for per-\/\+CPU data. It takes offsets and sizes for each area and creates them. Returns an array of \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} pointers.

pcpu\+\_\+get\+\_\+vm\+\_\+areas -\/ allocate vmalloc areas for percpu allocator @offsets:\+ array containing offset of each area @sizes:\+ array containing size of each area @nr\+\_\+vms:\+ the number of areas to allocate @align:\+ alignment, all entries in @offsets and @sizes must be aligned to this

Returns:\+ kmalloc\textquotesingle{}d \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} pointer array pointing to allocated vm\+\_\+structs on success, NULL on failure

Percpu allocator wants to use congruent vm areas so that it can maintain the offsets among percpu areas. This function allocates congruent vmalloc areas for it with GFP\+\_\+\+KERNEL. These areas tend to be scattered pretty far, distance between two areas easily going up to gigabytes. To avoid interacting with regular vmallocs, these areas are allocated from top.

Despite its complicated look, this allocator is rather simple. It does everything top-\/down and scans free blocks from the end looking for matching base. While scanning, if any of the areas do not fit the base address is pulled down to fit the area. Scanning is repeated till all the areas fit and then all necessary data structures are inserted and the result is returned.

Function Description:\+ Allocates multiple congruent vmalloc areas for percpu allocator. It scans from the top of the vmalloc space and allocates aligned areas. Returns an array of vm\+\_\+structs.

int remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct vm\+\_\+area\+\_\+struct \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{vma}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{pgoff}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Maps a vmalloc area into a user-\/space VMA. It maps the entire vmalloc area starting from the given offset. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range -\/ map vmalloc pages to userspace @vma:\+ vma to cover (map full range of vma) @addr:\+ vmalloc memory @pgoff:\+ number of pages into addr before first page to map

Returns:\+ 0 for success, -\/Exxx on failure

This function checks that addr is a valid vmalloc\textquotesingle{}ed area, and that it is big enough to cover the vma. Will return failure if that criteria isn\textquotesingle{}t met.

Similar to remap\+\_\+pfn\+\_\+range() (see mm/\+memory.\+c)

Function Description:\+ Maps a vmalloc area to userspace for the full VMA range. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a4cf06feeca551e895c56816fa2aad779}{remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+partial()}. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a4cf06feeca551e895c56816fa2aad779} 
int remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+partial (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct vm\+\_\+area\+\_\+struct \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{vma}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{uaddr}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{kaddr}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{pgoff}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Maps a portion of a vmalloc area into a user-\/space VMA. It creates a page table mapping from the vmalloc area to user space. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

remap\+\_\+vmalloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+partial -\/ map vmalloc pages to userspace @vma:\+ vma to cover @uaddr:\+ target user address to start at @kaddr:\+ virtual address of vmalloc kernel memory @pgoff:\+ offset from @kaddr to start at @size:\+ size of map area

Returns:\+ 0 for success, -\/Exxx on failure

This function checks that @kaddr is a valid vmalloc\textquotesingle{}ed area, and that it is big enough to cover the range starting at @uaddr in @vma. Will return failure if that criteria isn\textquotesingle{}t met.

Similar to remap\+\_\+pfn\+\_\+range() (see mm/\+memory.\+c)

Function Description:\+ Maps a portion of a vmalloc area to userspace. It validates the area and inserts pages into the VMA. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_ae57ef71b686f9e0fa536cd948632082f} 
struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} remove\+\_\+vm\+\_\+area (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Removes a \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} area from the vmalloc address space. It finds the area by address, removes it from the tree, and returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}. Returns NULL if not found.

remove\+\_\+vm\+\_\+area -\/ find and remove a continuous kernel virtual area @addr:\+ base address

Search for the kernel VM area starting at @addr, and remove it. This function returns the found VM area, but using it is NOT safe on SMP machines, except for its size or flags.

Return:\+ the area descriptor on success or NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Finds and removes a continuous kernel virtual area by address. It unlinks the \doxylink{structvmap__area}{vmap\+\_\+area} and returns the \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct}. Used during vfree.

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed array memory using vmalloc without allocation profiling. It multiplies n \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} size with overflow checking and zeroes the memory. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_aeaec68fe396182f9f32aee1de69d9b8f}{vcalloc()}. \Hypertarget{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}\index{vmalloc.h@{vmalloc.h}!vfree@{vfree}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23} 
void vfree (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Frees memory allocated with \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} or related functions. It returns the memory to the vmalloc subsystem. This is the main deallocation function for vmalloc memory.

vfree -\/ Release memory allocated by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} @addr:\+ Memory base address

Free the virtually continuous memory area starting at @addr, as obtained from one of the \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} family of APIs. This will usually also free the physical memory underlying the virtual allocation, but that memory is reference counted, so it will not be freed until the last user goes away.

If @addr is NULL, no operation is performed.

Context:\+ May sleep if called {\itshape not} from interrupt context. Must not be called in NMI context (strictly speaking, it could be if we have CONFIG\+\_\+\+ARCH\+\_\+\+HAVE\+\_\+\+NMI\+\_\+\+SAFE\+\_\+\+CMPXCHG, but making the calling conventions for \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} arch-\/dependent would be a really bad idea).

Function Description:\+ Frees memory allocated by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} or related functions. It removes the vm\+\_\+area, frees physical pages, and releases the virtual address space. This is the main deallocation function for vmalloc.

Function Description:\+ Frees vmalloc memory in atomic context. This is a safe version of \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} that can be called from interrupt context. It uses a deferred free mechanism.

vfree\+\_\+atomic -\/ release memory allocated by \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} @addr:\+ memory base address

This one is just like \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} but can be called in any atomic context except NMIs.

Description:\+ Frees vmalloc memory from atomic context. It adds the address to the deferred list and schedules a work. Must not be called from NMI context.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vm\_map\_ram()}{vm\_map\_ram()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_acd02620225f5b290f8935dbc1e45b18e} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct page \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{pages}{, }\item[{unsigned int}]{count}{, }\item[{int}]{node}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Maps a set of pages into a contiguous virtual address space. It takes an array of pages and maps them into RAM space. Returns the virtual address or NULL on failure.

vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram -\/ map pages linearly into kernel virtual address (vmalloc space) @pages:\+ an array of pointers to the pages to be mapped @count:\+ number of pages @node:\+ prefer to allocate data structures on this node

If you use this function for less than VMAP\+\_\+\+MAX\+\_\+\+ALLOC pages, it could be faster than vmap so it\textquotesingle{}s good. But if you mix long-\/life and short-\/life objects with \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_acd02620225f5b290f8935dbc1e45b18e}{vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram()}, it could consume lots of address space through fragmentation (especially on a 32bit machine). You could see failures in the end. Please use this function for short-\/lived objects.

Returns:\+ a pointer to the address that has been mapped, or NULL on failure

Function Description:\+ Maps pages linearly into kernel virtual address space (vmalloc space). It uses vmap\+\_\+blocks for small mappings and alloc\+\_\+vmap\+\_\+area for large mappings. Returns the virtual address or NULL. 256K with 4K pages

indicates vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram area

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vm\_unmap\_aliases()}{vm\_unmap\_aliases()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a80f7345092c599bf5a7648045113ae99} 
void vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+aliases (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Forces the unmapping of all pending vmalloc aliases. This flushes any cached mappings that might be pending. Used to ensure all mappings are removed.

vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+aliases -\/ unmap outstanding lazy aliases in the vmap layer

The vmap/\+vmalloc layer lazily flushes kernel virtual mappings primarily to amortize TLB flushing overheads. What this means is that any page you have now, may, in a former life, have been mapped into kernel virtual address by the vmap layer and so there might be some CPUs with TLB entries still referencing that page (additional to the regular 1:\+1 kernel mapping).

vm\+\_\+unmap\+\_\+aliases flushes all such lazy mappings. After it returns, we can be sure that none of the pages we have control over will have any aliases from the vmap layer.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps outstanding lazy aliases in the vmap layer. It flushes all pending lazy mappings to ensure no stale TLB entries remain. Used before freeing pages that may have vmap aliases.

vmalloc\+\_\+32 -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory (32bit addressable) @size:\+ allocation size

Function Description:\+ Allocates virtually contiguous memory addressable by 32-\/bit devices. It uses GFP\+\_\+\+VMALLOC32 flags. Returns the address or NULL.

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator and map them into contiguous kernel virtual space. If @size is greater than or equal to PMD\+\_\+\+SIZE, allow using huge pages for the memory

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Maps an array of pages into a contiguous virtual address space. It creates a mapping with the specified flags and protection. Returns the virtual address or NULL on failure.

vmap -\/ map an array of pages into virtually contiguous space @pages:\+ array of page pointers @count:\+ number of pages to map @flags:\+ vm\+\_\+area-\/\texorpdfstring{$>$}{>}flags @prot:\+ page protection for the mapping

Maps @count pages from @pages into contiguous kernel virtual space. If @flags contains VM\+\_\+\+MAP\+\_\+\+PUT\+\_\+\+PAGES the ownership of the pages array itself (which must be kmalloc or vmalloc memory) and one reference per pages in it are transferred from the caller to \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}, and will be freed /\+ dropped when \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} is called on the return value.

Return:\+ the address of the area or NULL on failure

Function Description:\+ Maps an array of pages into virtually contiguous space. It creates a vm\+\_\+area and maps the pages. Returns the virtual address or NULL.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a00e002a837662a6657c266ca9a997a2e} 
int vmap\+\_\+pages\+\_\+range (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{addr}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{end}{, }\item[{pgprot\+\_\+t}]{prot}{, }\item[{struct page \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{pages}{, }\item[{unsigned int}]{page\+\_\+shift}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Maps a range of pages into a virtual address range. It creates page table mappings for the specified address range using the given pages and page\+\_\+shift. Returns 0 on success or an error code.

vmap\+\_\+pages\+\_\+range -\/ map pages to a kernel virtual address @addr:\+ start of the VM area to map @end:\+ end of the VM area to map (non-\/inclusive) @prot:\+ page protection flags to use @pages:\+ pages to map (always PAGE\+\_\+\+SIZE pages) @page\+\_\+shift:\+ maximum shift that the pages may be mapped with, @pages must be aligned and contiguous up to at least this shift.

RETURNS:\+ 0 on success, -\/errno on failure.

Function Description:\+ Maps pages to virtual addresses with cache flushing. This is the main function for mapping pages into kernel virtual address space. Returns 0 on success or a negative error code.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vread\_iter()}{vread\_iter()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_ad9f5fb23122792d02fb4b9a116bb552d} 
long vread\+\_\+iter (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct iov\+\_\+iter \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{iter}{, }\item[{const char \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{addr}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{count}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

for /\+proc/\+kcore

Function Description:\+ Reads from vmalloc memory into an iov\+\_\+iter for /\+proc/\+kcore. It copies data from the vmalloc area to the user iterator. Returns the number of bytes read.

\doxylink{vmalloc_8c_ad9f5fb23122792d02fb4b9a116bb552d}{vread\+\_\+iter()} -\/ read vmalloc area in a safe way to an iterator. @iter:\+ the iterator to which data should be written. @addr:\+ vm address. @count:\+ number of bytes to be read.

This function checks that addr is a valid vmalloc\textquotesingle{}ed area, and copy data from that area to a given buffer. If the given memory range of \+[addr...addr+count) includes some valid address, data is copied to proper area of @buf. If there are memory holes, they\textquotesingle{}ll be zero-\/filled. IOREMAP area is treated as memory hole and no copy is done.

If \+[addr...addr+count) doesn\textquotesingle{}t includes any intersects with alive \doxylink{structvm__struct}{vm\+\_\+struct} area, returns 0. @buf should be kernel\textquotesingle{}s buffer.

Note:\+ In usual ops, vread() is never necessary because the caller should know \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} area is valid and can use memcpy(). This is for routines which have to access vmalloc area without any information, as /\+proc/\+kcore.

Return:\+ number of bytes for which addr and buf should be increased (same number as @count) or \%0 if \+[addr...addr+count) doesn\textquotesingle{}t include any intersection with valid vmalloc area

Function Description:\+ Reads from a vmalloc area to an iterator safely. It handles memory holes, IOREMAP areas, and sparse areas. Returns the number of bytes read. mark out the \doxylink{structvmap__block}{vmap\+\_\+block} sub-\/type

indicates vm\+\_\+map\+\_\+ram area

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vrealloc\_node\_align\_noprof()}{vrealloc\_node\_align\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a5e8f69c662b265347214d2aa20e6a490} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\+\_\+\+\_\+must\+\_\+check vrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{, }\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{align}{, }\item[{gfp\+\_\+t}]{flags}{, }\item[{int}]{nid}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Reallocates memory using vmalloc/\+vfree with alignment and node affinity without allocation profiling. It handles resizing of vmalloc-\/allocated memory, preserving contents up to the smaller size. This is the internal implementation for vrealloc with alignment.

vrealloc\+\_\+node\+\_\+align -\/ reallocate virtually contiguous memory; contents remain unchanged {\ttfamily }:\+ object to reallocate memory for @size:\+ the size to reallocate @align:\+ requested alignment @flags:\+ the flags for the page level allocator @nid:\+ node number of the target node

If {\ttfamily is} NULL, vrealloc\+\_\+\+XXX() behaves exactly like vmalloc\+\_\+\+XXX(). If @size is 0 and {\ttfamily is} not a NULL pointer, the object pointed to is freed.

If the caller wants the new memory to be on specific node {\itshape only}, \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+THISNODE flag should be set, otherwise the function will try to avoid reallocation and possibly disregard the specified @nid.

If \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO logic is requested, callers must ensure that, starting with the initial memory allocation, every subsequent call to this API for the same memory allocation is flagged with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Otherwise, it is possible that \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO is not fully honored by this API.

Requesting an alignment that is bigger than the alignment of the existing allocation will fail.

In any case, the contents of the object pointed to are preserved up to the lesser of the new and old sizes.

This function must not be called concurrently with itself or \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} for the same memory allocation.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory; NULL if @size is zero or in case of failure

Function Description:\+ Reallocates virtually contiguous memory with alignment and node affinity. It preserves existing contents and handles size changes. Returns the new address or NULL.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()} mapping and frees the virtual address space. It removes the mapping for the given address. This is the counterpart to \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}.

vunmap -\/ release virtual mapping obtained by \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()} @addr:\+ memory base address

Free the virtually contiguous memory area starting at @addr, which was created from the page array passed to \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}.

Must not be called in interrupt context.

Function Description:\+ Frees a virtual mapping created by \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}. It removes the vm\+\_\+area without freeing pages. Used to unmap pages mapped with \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a6abc66bc01119bd5e7bc61dd8f3c6274}{vmap()}.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a range of virtual addresses in the vmalloc space. It tears down page table mappings for the specified range.

vunmap\+\_\+range -\/ unmap kernel virtual addresses @addr:\+ start of the VM area to unmap @end:\+ end of the VM area to unmap (non-\/inclusive)

Clears any present PTEs in the virtual address range, flushes TLBs and caches. Any subsequent access to the address before it has been re-\/mapped is a kernel bug.

Function Description:\+ Unmaps a range of virtual addresses with cache and TLB flushing. It calls flush\+\_\+cache\+\_\+vunmap(), \doxylink{vmalloc_8c_a80a3260928d403f4980a094110e6eac2}{vunmap\+\_\+range\+\_\+noflush()}, and flush\+\_\+tlb\+\_\+kernel\+\_\+range(). This is the main function for unmapping kernel virtual addresses.

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory on a specific NUMA node without allocation profiling. It allocates zeroed memory from the specified node. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a5a98e640b51e42b458cd21d2b92e60f7}{vzalloc\+\_\+node()}.

vzalloc\+\_\+node -\/ allocate memory on a specific node with zero fill @size:\+ allocation size @node:\+ numa node

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator and map them into contiguous kernel virtual space. The memory allocated is set to zero.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory on a specific node. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a3b2d920abf792e259839419e3d968148}{vmalloc\+\_\+node()} with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Returns the address or NULL.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{vzalloc\_noprof()}{vzalloc\_noprof()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{vmalloc_8h_a64f7afd1e1788467018eb5b9d3c518f3} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} vzalloc\+\_\+noprof (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [extern]}}

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory without allocation profiling. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} and zeroes the allocated memory. This is the internal implementation for \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a5868566242b769d58025e333da614bee}{vzalloc()}.

vzalloc -\/ allocate virtually contiguous memory with zero fill @size:\+ allocation size

Allocate enough pages to cover @size from the page level allocator and map them into contiguous kernel virtual space. The memory allocated is set to zero.

For tight control over page level allocator and protection flags use \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_abf4d6ac386bd660cbdd449b5cfefc11d}{\+\_\+\+\_\+vmalloc()} instead.

Return:\+ pointer to the allocated memory or NULL on error

Function Description:\+ Allocates zeroed virtually contiguous memory. It calls \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a4fa760a1e23c4b642add844f040ae713}{vmalloc()} with \+\_\+\+\_\+\+GFP\+\_\+\+ZERO. Returns the address or NULL.
